ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u242	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Kukiz Club15 a position on the parliamentary draft law amending the law on the protection of the rights of tenants, the housing stock of the commune and on the amendment of the Civil Code, seym prints No. 74 and 90. The draft law amending the law on the protection of the rights of tenants, the housing stock of the municipality and on the amendment of the Civil Code, hereinafter referred to as the project, provides for the amendment of the article. 32 Act of 21 June 2001 on the protection of the rights of tenants, the housing stock of the commune and on the amendment of the Civil Code. The proposed amendment aims to ensure that tenants have the right to a replacement and to cover removal costs due to the need for renovation or demolition of a building that needs to be emptied. This obligation rests with the competent municipality. The law on the protection of tenants' rights has set the date 31 December 2015 as a limit for the application of this provision. Extension of this provision, as applicants want, by 10 years, i.e. By 31 December 2025, it shall be unfounded. Putting aside the target problem ad Kalendas Graecas is de facto putting aside the deadline that will never happen. With satisfaction, Mr President, I welcome the proposal of the applicant's self-correction to reduce this deadline to 2 years, i.e. until 31 December 2017. However, when we prepared the speech, we had information about the 10-year deadline. The government of the Civic Platform and the Polish People's Party in the end of the previous term of office of the Sejm apparently missed the upcoming term of the bill. No consultation has been carried out, ladies and gentlemen, with the municipalities under the statutory obligation to provide tenants with the right to a replacement and to cover the removal costs due to the need for renovation or demolition of the building. The financial implications of the extension of this provision have not been established. The draft was also not consulted on the joint committee of government and local government. The Commissions of Infrastructure and Local Government at their joint meeting on 10 December 2015 did not receive the information necessary to make a proper decision on whether to recommend to the High House an extension of the provision indefinitely, as the Civic Platform stated in the abandoned project, or to limit its validity within a period of reasonable grounds, or may not prolong its validity at all, as the local authorities would have liked, as the municipalities would have wanted. In these circumstances, applicants have proposed to extend this provision in the dark by 10 years. This solution will delay the subject, which, for two terms of ten years, will not need to be dealt with. However, this case is very concerned by the local government units which must fulfil their obligation. The practice shows that the existing solution protects tenants, but gives rise to the risk that residential owners will deliberately neglect the state of the property in order to quickly decapitalise and force the move of tenants and provide them with a replacement place at the expense of the municipality. This is because it is the owners of premises for which the regulated rent was in force at the date of the entry into force of the Act, and therefore they have real difficulties in obtaining from the rental funds to meet the obligation to maintain the buildings in good technical condition. The forced temporary situation, as a result of forced removal of tenants, often lasts for years. The proposed change, objectively speaking, will have an impact on the budgets of local self-government units, but, as the applicant pointed out, it is not possible to estimate it because the number of buildings owned by natural persons who were settled in administrative mode years ago is not known, nor is their technical condition known. However, in this case the previous PO-PSL government, which did not deal with the case in time, should be held responsible for the situation. Kraków had 2 premises, in 2012 there were 12, then 4, 5 and 5 premises in 2015. Warsaw had 80 premises in 2014, this year in the first half of the year there were 7 premises. The Łódź had 66 replacement premises delivered last year, in 2013 there were 38 of them. So when we look at these numbers, and some of these locations are turned off, because they're supplied, so to speak, as a result of other regulations, we're going to find that this is not a big financial problem. In addition, we will stand in the course of the discussion, the state and we as a government, before the question of assessing these costs. Is this the value of the rent that one tenant has to pay instead of the other? I don't think so, 'cause no matter which tenant would pay it. Is this the value of the place that the municipality has to deliver? That is also hard to say, because the municipalities have never proved that they had to buy a place to do so. They would have to show that they bought a place of such value by doing this duty. There's no record of that either. I think that the municipalities do not buy premises to exercise powers or obligations under this law, so it will be very difficult to estimate these costs. The replacement location for the time of renovation or demolition is delivered by the municipality, and then, after the renovation or after the construction of the new premises, the owner rents the premises on the free market and does not need to acquire those tenants back. Perhaps it is necessary to introduce such a solution, so that it is an obligation for the owner to offer those tenants, which he, in turn, disposed of back to those premises. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u268	Thank you very much. Dear Marshal! High Izbo! It is my great pleasure to present a draft resolution on commemorating the 130th anniversary of Adam Chętnik's birth. The draft resolution was unanimously approved by the Committee on Culture and Media. I will add that Adam Chętnik was also honoured by the local government of Podlaskie Voivodeship and the year 2015 is the year to which Adam Chętnik patronizes, because it is certainly one of the most interesting people on the border of Kurpiowsko-Mazurskie. If we're talking about Kurpies, he was just one of the greatest characters, he was called Ambassador of the Kurpie environment. A few data on his résumé. Born in 1885 in Novgorod near Lomza. He started his education even before World War I when he published a country-based, ethnographic and historical writing for young people. He was also involved in social activities, establishing a contribution library and organizing secret teaching. He promoted cooperative movement, actively participated in the work of joining Mazur to Poland, engaging in preparations for the plebiscite. After Poland regained its independence in 1918, he became a Member of the land of Łomża at the First Sejm. In 1923 he was awarded The Knight's Cross of the Order of Polonia Restituta and the Golden Cross of Merit. He opened the Kurpiowski Museum in the open air in Novgorod in 1927. Now it's a scan of his name. He organized a scientific institution called the Science and Research Station of the Central Narwi basin, which carried out scientific activities on the north-eastern lands of Poland. During World War II, all the museum and scientific achievements gathered in Novgorod were destroyed. After the war, Adam Chętnik started rebuilding these facilities, and in 1948, thanks to his efforts, the Regional Museum was opened in Łomża. It currently functions as the North-Mazowieckie Museum. Adam Chętnik is the author of many publications devoted to Kurpiom. Its scientific achievements include more than 100 works and about 500 articles. At the end of his life he was awarded the Officer's Cross of the Order of the Rebirth of Poland for his merits for regional museums. Highly valued in the scientific community has been recognised as a precursor in the field of research and dissemination of folk culture. The museum in Novgorod was the second open air museum in the country after the Kashubian Ethnographic Park. Adam Chętnik remained faithful to his ideals, scientific and research passions and museum interests according to his life's credo: By doing, I wonder if it would be good for Poland. I think that in the case of such resolutions the Sejm is usually unanimous and unanimous, unanimously adopts such resolutions, honoring such outstanding figures. I think and hope it's the same this time. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u269	Thank you very much, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear three minutes' statements on behalf of the clubs. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u270	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I have the honour to present my position on the parliamentary draft resolution on the 130th anniversary of Adam Chętnik's birth. He was one of the prominent cultural activists, ethnographer, forerunner of scientific research on Kurpie culture, museum, social and political activist, member of the Polish Academy of Skills. He also created poetry, he was an amateur musician playing many instruments. Its scientific achievements include more than 100 works, about 500 articles, several monographical titles. He started his educational activity before World War I when he wrote magazines for rural youth on the subject of local, ethnographic and historical issues. Later, he also engaged in social activities. After Poland regained independence in 1918, he was a Member of the land of Łomża at the First Sejm, as mentioned by my previous speaker. As a scientist and Member of the Second Republic of Poland, he made a major contribution to securing the material culture of the Kurpi region, creating open-air museums and research institutions. It is worth mentioning here the opinion in the article entitled The merit of Adam Chętnik in spreading the ideals of the 'Kurpie region'. The author of this article is his granddaughter Ewa Chętnik-Donatowicz. The struggle of Adam Chętnik for historical truth and cultural identity took on the nature of the struggle for Polishness throughout the life. He spoke as Kurp-Slavian in favour of the values that the stream of historical events carried with him. He was convinced that defending culture was no less important than defending freedom, independence and national pride. He fought for the Polish school in the Russian partition, he was an activist among rural youth, author of popular and scientific works on the life of people on Kurpia and one of the first museums in the museum, creating Skansen Kurpiowski in Nowogrod. He was convinced that the Kurpie and Mazury people were together, so he was actively involved in preparations for the plebiscite in Mazury in 1920. Thanks to his active social publicity, he was elected as a Member of the Sejm in the years 1922 and 1927. However, after the end of this one term, he withdrew from politics and devoted himself primarily to culture and museums. The work of Chętnik is a valuable source for cultural researchers, especially ethnographers. It can be said that these works have seriously saved Kurpie land from oblivion. For Poland, he has done a great deal, primarily defending Polishness in Warmia and Mazury and taking care of cultural heritage in Kurpia. Given the above, I am pleased to announce that the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club will support the relevant draft resolution. Thank you. [[Applause]]

He is the author of over 100 scientific works, including books dedicated to the Green Forest Kurpy. In Novgorod he founded Skansen Kurpiowski, who now bears his name. The educational, social and scientific activities began in early youth, including secret teaching and establishing the first library or local music and theatre band in Novgorod. He was also actively involved in resisting the russification of education. He continued his scientific interest in social and patriotic activities at a mature age. He was a member of the Polish Academy of Skills, President of the newly established Puszczański Union, during the plebiscite in Warmia and Mazury, he actively supported the joining of Mazury to Poland and then against Germanization and for maintaining the national consciousness of the Mazury brothers. He devoted his next history-ethnographic work to this subject, the most important of which were the books of "Pusza Kurpiowska" and "Kurpiowska Chata" and "Mazurski" by the route. The activities of the Volunteer for Mazury and their population were the reason for repression by the occupation authorities during World War II. The crowning of Adam Chętnik's political activities was the exercise of a mandate, as my esteemed previous speakers, a Member of the Parliament of the Republic of Poland, the first term of office, 1922 and 1927, on behalf of the People's-National Union. The rich, diverse publication and organizing activity of Adam Chętnik puts it among the most famous researchers and popularizers of Polish culture in the 20th century. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland on the 130th anniversary of the birth of Adam Chętnik honors the memory of him and his achievements. It is my great pleasure to inform you that the Modern Club will support the resolution. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Poland, when joining the European Union, committed itself to fulfilling, among others, requirements for urban waste water systems and treatment plants resulting from Council Directive 91/271/EEC, in accordance with the provisions of the Accession Treaty. The transitional period for the implementation of the Wastewater Directive expires on 31 December 2015 and therefore penalties for failure to comply with the requirements of the Waste Water Directive would have to be imposed from 1 January 2016. The legal basis for the calculation of environmental penalties by the operator of the municipal waste water treatment plant is Article. Point (a) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 1a Act Environmental Law. It must be borne in mind that according to the article. 292 Environmental law in the absence of a permit required, the environmental operator shall bear fees increased by 500% for the introduction of waste water into water or to land. During the previous term of office of the Sejm, the Committee on the Environment, Natural Resources and Forestry addressed the denierate on the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Programme - the date of this document is 10 July 2013 - to the then Minister of the Environment. The Commission was concerned about the results of the Supreme Audit Chamber's audit on the state of implementation of the According to this report, the NIK predicted that only 31% of the total pollution charge would be cleaned at the end of the transitional period, i.e. 31 December 2015. On the basis of the data collected up to the fourth update of the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Programme, the investment needs of municipalities in meeting the requirements of Council Directive 91/271/EEC amount to a total of approximately PLN 28 450 million. The construction of the necessary sewerage facilities is a great financial effort for municipal authorities, for which it is their own task, I remind you, and therefore the levying of environmental and administrative fines in this situation is not intended, even I would say, counterproductive, because it would reduce the resources they should allocate to the municipalities for the construction of sewage treatment plants and sewage treatment. For various reasons, financial, random and organisational, the completion of investments in the water economy carried out by the municipalities is prolonged. According to the investment plans submitted by the agglomeration, after 31 December 2015, 91 new waste water treatment plants and other investments are planned, e.g. reconstruction of the sedimentary part and modernization of sewage treatment plants in 651 treatment plants. In particular, for small municipalities, the task of achieving an environmental effect, despite EU funding, is a task that exceeds their financial capacity, so the imposition of increased charges may significantly impede the implementation of planned or initiated investments. It is therefore proposed to introduce legislative changes extending the period of delay in the collection of increased environmental charges for the water and sewage economy, changes in environmental law. High Izbo! Revision of the article. Point (a) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 1a The environmental law contained in the Marshal's draft of the Act refers to the change of the deadline for deferring payment of the environmental and administrative penalty payment, provided that the project which will be implemented to remove the reasons for the increased fees or penalties is included in the update of the National Urban Waste Water Treatment Scheme and if this is implemented by 31 December 2018. The amendment to the Environmental Law will enable the municipalities to complete their investments and will not result in an additional financial burden on those entities which carry out the projects concerned. These are municipalities or municipal establishments that deal with the water and sewage economy on behalf of the municipalities. High Izbo! Article 4 is replaced by the following: The 3rd draft law will allow the competent authority to speed up the decision to cancel payments to entities which have implemented the necessary sewage and sewage treatment facilities. The legislative amendment relating to the entities which carried out the projects by 31 December 2015 will further motivate obliged entities to take environmental action. Art. 2 The Act refers to the charge for the introduction of gases or dust into the air charged on the basis of the provisions of the Law of 17 July 2009 on the greenhouse gas emission management system and other substances. The system for recording these charges should be uniform, used by all marshal offices and combined with the national greenhouse gas and other information base. This system hasn't worked properly so far. It cannot be the same as it is often in tax matters that taxes are only paid by honest people or by people who are not smart enough. The aim is to ensure that the system applies equally to all operators and ensures good standard and consistency so that all operators obliged to pay these fees are treated equally. This equal treatment is ultimately a fundamental distinction between the rule of law. It is therefore proposed to change the deadlines set out in Article in relation to these problems and obstacles. 60a of this law and the extension of the entry into force of the statutory regulations in this respect to 1 January 2019, i.e. 3 years. It is proposed for the whole of the draft contained in the committee's report, i.e. in print No 109, the entry into force of the Act in question as of 30 December 2015. High Izbo! The entry into force of the proposed amendments to this Act will not result in an increase in expenditure in the budget of the State and in the budget of local government units. In the draft law, amendments 13, 14, 15, 16 concern water law permits. A proposal has been made to transfer some of the issues covered by water permits to the notification. Let me remind you that this is a kind of analogy to building law, where certain things require building permits, certain types of activities require building permits, certain activities require notification. Similarly, less complex actions are foreseen for the notification procedure here. This should reduce the administrative burden. The provisions of the draft law modify the catalogue of actions requiring water law permits and define a catalogue of actions which do not require authorisation but require notification to the competent authority. A catalogue of activities that do not require a water permit shall also be modified. Proposed provision of the Article. 123a Water law therefore sets out the list of activities to be notified to the authority competent to issue the permit, the scope of the notification, the procedure for making that notification, the procedure for objecting by the authority competent to issue the water law permit and similar provisions which standardise the issue. These are simply precious things, in the most basic sense of the word, and they are the source of this pressure. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u308	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on amending the Act amending the Water Law Act. The draft law amending the Water Law Act has many advantages. Its deregulation character is essential, e.g. transfer to the level of local authorities the possibility of recognition, not the compulsory inclusion of maps defining the extent of flood areas. The positive nature of this solution is that local authorities do not have to accept mechanically determined, according to procedures which do not take into account local experience and spatial and natural state, boundaries of such areas. It is often the case that due to regulatory infrastructure solutions, e.g. the construction of flood protections, water reservoirs or already existing buildings, a different state of protection and flood safety than it results from maps developed on the basis of mechanically used designs. However, the introduction of optional measures to take account of flood risk maps does not mean that local authorities will act freely. For, there are many provisions concerning, for example, the rules for the adoption of a study and a spatial planning plan that take into account flood safety. The second positive element of the proposed amendment is the identification of cases of activities which do not require water permits, but only notifications to be undertaken. This will limit the freedom of local officials. What raises the greatest doubts in the proposed amendment to the Water Law Act is the introduction of permanent administrative arrangements for appointment as president of the National Water Management Board and regional water management directors. This enables the instrumental management of these actors. However, it may be comforting that it will not be worse than during the PO-PSL coalition, where, under the appearance of objectivity of competitions, these functions included incompetent persons and hindering the use of resources and properties at the disposal of regional water management boards, e.g. in the case of the construction of a tank in Świnna Poręba and existing in Dobczyce. I myself, as well as Kukiz15, will vote in favour of the proposed amendments. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

Indeed, it is about this aspect. Today, the environmental committee has been discussed most widely, because the spatial map of flood risks is an essential instrument for flood protection. This seems to be an attempt to push responsibility from government to government in the event of a natural disaster. Because who will be responsible for the damage that will be caused to private individuals, to legal persons as a result of a natural disaster, if for this decision 100% Will the local government answer? Poles should feel safe and have the State's guarantee on security related to the often investing of their living achievements, therefore Polish People's Party absolutely critically refers to this recording. Another question, also raised today, concerns a rather temporary legal solution related to allowing the Prime Minister to appoint a person acting as President of the National Water Management Board and similar powers in the regions. Here's what I'm gonna say. Apart from the fact that it leads, it gives the opportunity to create a gateway to taking very bad practices, I do not recall, I do not know in our legal order a law which would regulate in such a unit way, was a unitary regulation. After all, statutory regulation should be a system regulation, and here we are talking about a specific case, a specific institution and a specific function. That is why we are also looking at this proposal as a negative one. Therefore, the Polish People's Party proposes two amendments - repealing the questionable articles I was talking about. The vote is, of course, dependent on acceptance or no amendments tabled by us. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u348	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! In accordance with the order of the Marshal, the Health Commission carried out the first reading of the parliamentary draft law amending the Law on the amendment of the Law on the provision of health care financed by public funds. This project is included in print No 73. The essence of this project is to postpone the contractual deadline for medical services to 30 June 2017. This shift is not automatic and requires the action of interested parties, i.e. on the one hand the conclusions of the Regional Branch Directors of the National Health Fund, on the other hand the consent of the healthcare providers. Three reasons can be mentioned for the need to postpone this deadline. Firstly, the so-called ones are not yet developed. maps of health needs, which are intended to rationalise and make health care organisation more effective in Poland and to make public health funds spent effectively. It is quite clear that the new contracting process should be carried out after the adoption of such maps of health needs. Secondly, there is a need for critical verification of the valuation of medical procedures, which are criticised by various interested environments. Thirdly, this new contracting process needs to be managed, organised and prepared. In addition, the draft law extends the deadline for defining new contract rules. It shall postpone that period until 1 July 2016. The corresponding 600-page regulation was Whereas the internal legislative procedure has not yet been completed. ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u354	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to present the position of the Kukiz Club15 on the draft law amending the Law on the revision of the Law on healthcare services financed by public funds and certain other laws. On 22 July 2014, an amendment to the Law on the revision of the Law on the provision of public-funded healthcare provision and certain other laws was adopted to extend the duration of healthcare contracts in the various areas, but for a period not exceeding 30 June 2016. On that day, the first maps of health needs are to be introduced resulting from the project of the Ministry of Health. The essence of the project is that, on the basis of the data from the contracts previously signed and information on the location of the establishments, it is necessary to determine in which direction to develop and change contracting in the region in question in the coming years. Changes in health policy planning are intended to ensure access to benefits based on regional health needs maps drawn up every 5 years. The extension of existing contracts was intended to give time to properly prepare competition proceedings on the basis of new rules. As long as the idea of organizing the medical services market is good, the suspension of the contract is no longer. We're talking about 2014. The suspension of the contract gave rise to legitimate concerns among the heads and founders of medical establishments modernising their bases with a view to entering the competition and signing contracts with the public payer, while the second year in a row was to be deprived of the possibility of seeking to finance health care benefits from the National Health Fund. The second year, because in 2013, also the Act of 11 October, was suspended from contracting. The National Health Fund used the powers granted by the Act and annexed the contracts until March 2016, announcing the announcement of a new competition in autumn this year. This gave the basis for optimistic thinking about the future for those who survived without contracts with a public payer. There are also others who trust in legal stability, who invest in health care. In the autumn of this year, the public payer, instead of announcing the competition, once again exercised the rights given to him by the law and extended the suspension of the contract for another three months until June 2016, using the maximum time given to him by the law. Three consecutive months without a chance to obtain contracts from the National Health Fund can lead to the closure of facilities and deprived patients of access to benefits. The indexing of contracts has already become a rule, as it lasts for at least 2 years and sometimes 3 years. This state of affairs sanctiones contracts concluded by medicinal establishments on a privileged basis, while at the same time discriminates against new medicinal products by denying the possibility of applying for a contract. This is a violation of equal opportunities for development and activities of entities in the same sector. For example, in the Pomeranian voivodship there are districts where patients are deprived of access to medical facilities with contracts with the National Health Fund, because contracts signed with a privileged group of healthcare providers, which do not cover the scope of their entire voivodship, are annexed for several years and although new medical entities have been established in this area, patients still have to drive several dozens of kilometers, in extreme cases - to the clinic in the neighboring district, because only there will they receive public-funded healthcare services. This, of course, extends the waiting time for the benefit, and that is also not meaningless. This applies e.g. mental health and addiction treatment services. The Pomeranian Regional Branch of the National Health Fund in the last days of November of this year, i.e. several days ago, assured that the purchase plan of the Pomeranian Regional Branch of the National Health Fund for 2016 such as psychiatric care and addiction treatment is to carry out new competition procedures to conclude multi-annual health care contracts in force from 1 July 2016. He kept me informed at the same time, [[♪ The bell ♪]] that the announcement of planned proceedings may take place this year. However, a few days later, we are proceeding with a law which extends the contracts concluded several years earlier for the following year, preventing new healthcare providers from presenting offers for public-funded healthcare services. If the National Health Fund can't prepare competition proceedings in accordance with new rules...

ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u378	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to make a statement on the necessity of the construction of the Brest Kujawski ring road in Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship along with the link to the Pikutko motorway junction with Włocławek. Brest Kujawski is a small, picturesque town, the cradle of archaeology and medieval culture, and was the seat of the Dukes of Kujawski, including the restorer of the Kingdom of Poland Władysław Łokietka. A city that today suffers from intense traffic, especially a truck that runs through its center. Brest Kujawski crosses three district roads, three voivodship roads and a national road on the Warsaw - Poznań route. For a long time, the need to build a ring road and to rebuild a 4-kilometer section of the national road No.62 in order to create a fast and safe link between Włocławek and Brzescie Kujawski and the motorway junction in Pikutkow. The reconstruction of the National Road No 62 on the Włocławek-Brest Kujawski section was, in fact, included in the National Road Construction Programme 2014-2023, but the construction of the ring road was no longer taken into account. Local authorities and residents are increasingly desperate and bitter. The battle for the construction of the ring road has been going on for years and there is still no positive solution to this problem. The city's and county's Łódźrs called for numerous calls to include this investment in road construction plans, the Brest Kujawski Circuit Construction Support Association was established, pickets and meetings were organized, numerous publicizing actions were taken. The only rational solution is to treat this road investment holistically and with one investment effort to build both a section connecting the motorway with more than 100 thousand Włocławek, Brest Kujawski and the economic zone that was created under Brest and a bypass that will bring accumulated and increased traffic beyond this historic city. Financial considerations are also in favour of this task, because the total cost of the investment, which so well solves the communication problem in the south of Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship, is according to estimates of the General Directorate for National Roads and Motorways and the Brest self-government just 120 million PLN. It's a relatively small amount of money for road investments. The implementation of this investment would not only relieve the narrow streets of the city from too much traffic, but would also become an opportunity to accelerate the development of the entire Włocławska region through the dynamics of the Brest economic development zone - due to proper communication, the likelihood of attracting new investors and jobs would be significantly increased, which is a particularly important issue in a region facing very high unemployment. The possibility of financial participation of the Kujawsko-Pomorskie voivodship in the implementation of this investment is also in favour of placing this task in the road construction project. I appeal for the urgent integration of the construction of the Brest Kujawski ring road into the 2014-2023 national road construction programme, together with the reconstruction of the national road No 62 on section Włocławek - Brest Kujawski, and for the earliest possible start of construction of the connector itself, so that we do not wait until 2023 or longer. I would also suggest that in the road construction program, a pool of about 20 million PLN should be designated and documentation should be prepared for all such small investments in Poland, but very sensitive in solving congestion. Such documentation is valid for up to 10 years, and when we have it, it can always be activated in case of savings on tenders. It would be very economical and predictable. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u379	Thank you very much. That concludes our parliamentary statements.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u380	Declaration on the 45th anniversary of December'70 events: Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 14 and 22 December the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [3] laying down detailed rules for the application of Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial Regulation applicable to the general budget of the Union and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the Financial Regulation applicable to the general budget of the European Communities and repealing Council Regulation (EC, Euratom) No 1605/2002 on the The direct cause of the outbreak of social dissatisfaction was the drastic price increases introduced by the authorities several days before Christmas. Of course, it was just one of the manifestations of the political and economic crisis at the time, but it affected society so much that citizens decided to express their opposition loudly. Violent protests shocked coastal cities between 14 and 22 December 1970. The first strikes began at the yard. Lenin in Gdansk. The employees raised the demand for lower prices. Manifested under the building of the PZPR Voivodeship Committee. However, the voice of the demonstrators was not heard. Instead of trying to establish a dialogue, the communist authorities decided to attack the protestors, directing MO officers against them. Violent street fighting took place in Gdansk until late evening hours. Dozens of people were injured. Another day didn't bring peace of mind. The protests are getting worse. The law enforcement forces have started using anti-gun protesters. The first fatalities were reported in the vicinity of the MO City Command building. Both-sided aggression has intensified. The building which was the seat of the local party authorities was set on fire. On the streets of Gdansk there were soldiers armed with machine guns, as well as heavy equipment. Several people were killed, several hundred were injured. The next day, when the shipbuilders wanted to walk back to the streets of the city, they were shot at by militiamen and soldiers. The strike wave spilled into other coastal cities - Elbląg, Gdynia, Słupsk. The most violent riots took place in Szczecin. The riots began there on 17 December. Staff of the shipyard Adolf Warski was headed for the PZPR Voivodeship Committee. The protesters were brutally attacked by the militia. The seat of the communist authorities was set on fire. However, the most tragic events occurred that day in Gdynia. There the army and militia opened fire to helpless, working people who didn't even participate in street riots. At least 18 people lost their lives. The symbol of these events is the figure of an 18-year-old employee of Gdynia Shipyard - Zbigniew Godlewski, whose body of residents in gesture of discord carried the streets of the city. This incident was immortalized in the Ballad about Janek Wiśniewski. During a few days of December, tens of thousands of people took part in street clashes on the streets of the coastal cities. The main burden of fighting the protesters was taken over by militia officers. They were also supported by the army. To pacify the protests on the Coast, the authorities used a total of about 27 thousand. Soldiers and 550 tanks, 750 armored transporters and 2100 cars. According to official data in December'70, a total of 45 people were killed and 1,165 were injured. However, the size of riots and the number of forces directed against demonstrators allows us to suppose that these numbers are understated. In other parts of the country during this period there was a wave of strikes, increased flyers colportage and more prominent passwords directed against the PZPR. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2015-12-15-sejm-05-1.u381	I hereby order the meeting to be adjourned until 3 p.m. 9 today, 16 December 2015 [[Adjournment at the meeting at 3 p.m. 01 min 08]]
